# Build Graph

Bazel (via Bazelisk, pinned in `.bazelversion`) is the authoritative build:
kernel, checks, lints, and the bootable image are one graph
(`docs/lifecycle/02-build-and-test-infrastructure.md`). The cargo workspace
is the developer inner loop only; nothing cargo produces is shipped.

- `platforms/` — the bare-metal target platforms (`x86_64-kernel`;
  and `aarch64-kernel`, one bare (os:none) platform per CPU family; the
  architecture is a `tessera_kernel_binary` attribute, not a `select()`).
- `rules/platform_transition.bzl` — transitions kernel binaries to their
  platform so a plain host `bazel build //...` builds everything.
- `rules/kernel.bzl` — `tessera_kernel_binary`: rust_binary with the
  kernel codegen flags and linker script.

Common commands:

```bash
bazel test //...                  # tiers 0 + 1 + 3
bazel build //... --config=lint   # rustfmt + clippy, host-configurable targets
bazel test //tools/qemu:smoke_boot_test --config=kvm   # KVM-accelerated
bazel build //kernel/image:tessera_iso                 # bootable ISO
```

## Configuration

What a build of this tree *is* — the fixed-capacity tables `kcore` allocates,
the features compiled in, and the ring-3 programs each image carries — is
declared in `config/kernel.config` and chosen by a profile under
`config/profiles/`. `--//config:profile=<name>` reconfigures the whole
invocation; the cargo inner loop reads `TESSERA_PROFILE`. Adding a profile is
dropping a file into that directory: the list is globbed, not written down.

```bash
bazel build //kernel/kernel-aarch64 --//config:profile=aarch64-lean

kconfig=//tools/kconfig:kconfig                       # or: cargo run -p tessera-kconfig --
bazel run $kconfig -- menu   config/kernel.config config/profiles/default.profile
bazel run $kconfig -- show   config/kernel.config config/profiles/default.profile aarch64
bazel run $kconfig -- diff   config/kernel.config config/profiles/{default,aarch64-lean}.profile
bazel run $kconfig -- check  config/kernel.config config/profiles/*.profile
```

`menu` is interactive and wants a terminal; `show` prints the resolved
configuration with the provenance of every value. Nothing is clamped: a profile
that asks for something outside a declared range, or that breaks an invariant
one setting declares about another, does not build (deviation D199).

The ports and kernel binaries build behind a platform transition, which the
lint aspects do not cross (deviation D183). Each needs its own invocation, with
the crates named rather than globbed — a package wildcard would try to build the
host `rust_test` targets for bare metal:

```bash
bazel build //kernel/karch-x86_64  //kernel/kernel:kernel_bin                 --config=lint-x86_64
bazel build //kernel/karch-aarch64 //kernel/karch-arm-common \
            //kernel/kernel-aarch64:kernel-aarch64_bin                        --config=lint-aarch64
bazel build //kernel/karch-riscv64 //kernel/karch-riscv-common \
            //kernel/kernel-riscv64:kernel-riscv64_bin                        --config=lint-riscv64
bazel build //kernel/karch-riscv32 //kernel/karch-riscv-common \
            //kernel/kernel-riscv32:kernel-riscv32_bin                        --config=lint-riscv32
bazel build //kernel/karch-arm32   //kernel/karch-arm-common \
            //kernel/kernel-arm32:kernel-arm32_bin                            --config=lint-arm32
```

## Deviation Ledger

Documented gaps between this milestone and the normative build rules in
`docs/lifecycle/02-build-and-test-infrastructure.md` /
`docs/lifecycle/04-coding-guidelines.md`. Deviations are recorded, owned,
and carry exit criteria — never silently absorbed.

A number is unique and permanent; a row's *position* is where it was written.
The two can disagree — D54 sits beside the entry it continues, and D214 kept
its place in the sequence when it was renumbered off a collision — so a
deviation is found by its number, not by counting rows.
`//tools/checks:ledger_test` holds the parts of that a machine can check: one
row per number with no gaps, three cells in every row (an unescaped `|` in the
prose silently drops the exit criterion when this renders), no blank line
inside the table (which ends it, and once hid 180 rows), and every `D<n>`
cited anywhere in the tree resolving to a row.

| # | Deviation | Exit criterion |
|---|-----------|----------------|
| D1 | Kernel targets use the built-in `x86_64-unknown-none` spec (static relocation and kernel code model forced in `rules/kernel.bzl`) instead of a checked-in custom target JSON. | Custom target JSON lands with the CFI/shadow-stack hardening flags the security model requires. |
| D2 | `third_party/limine/` vendors prebuilt bootloader binaries, sha256-pinned in its METADATA. | Limine is built from source in the graph before any release artifact ships. |
| D3 | Rust toolchains are downloaded hash-verified from upstream (per `MODULE.bazel.lock`), not rebuilt from source. | Toolchain rebuild-from-source is a release-train requirement, not a Stage 0 one. |
| D4 | `xorriso` and `qemu-system-x86_64` come from host packages; the smoke test is tagged `no-remote`. | Both become hermetic toolchains when remote execution / CI workers arrive. |
| D5 | ~~No in-kernel test-image harness yet; arch-only logic is covered by the smoke boot.~~ (done, D63) The architecture-conformance battery (`kernel/arch-conformance`) is the in-kernel harness — both ports run it at boot and its verdicts gate the exit status. | ~~Lands with the next milestone.~~ |
| D6 | KASLR randomizes the kernel direct map to one of ~80 PML4 slots (512 GiB granularity) using RDRAND, or the timestamp counter when RDRAND is absent (a weak boot fallback). The boot stack stays mapped at the fixed bootloader HHDM base as a compatibility window; kernel text is not relocated. | Real kernel CSPRNG entropy, finer granularity, a fresh randomized boot stack, and kernel-text KASLR land with D1's hardened custom target. |
| D7 | Anonymous demand paging (lazy zero-fill on fault) and copy-on-write now resolve in-kernel; what remains is file/object-backed mappings served by the external pager, and measurement of B8 (anon fault) / B9 (COW). | File/object-backed mappings land with the external pager (D27); B8/B9 measured with the perf rig (D30). |
| D8 | ~~Single-core only.~~ **Dispatch: closed by D225** — every CPU the machine has comes online and runs threads off a run queue of its own (`smp.all-online`). **Shootdown: closed by D227** — `kcore::vm::invalidate` says which CPUs are left to tell and `kcore::shootdown` tells them. The set is exact for the kernel space (every online CPU) and still over-reports for others, where the mask is set on activate and never cleared. | Closed. What remains is an optimisation, not a correctness gap: clear the mask on switch-away so a space that stopped being active on a CPU is not shot down there, and narrow the flush from whole-TLB to a range. |
| D9 | The Stage-0 perf rig now **exists** and measures the context-switch (B7) and fault (B8) paths with serialized invariant-TSC timing and exact percentiles, reported at boot; under QEMU these validate the rig and catch regressions only (D34). | R1 bare-metal compliance for B1–B11 (D34). |
| D10 | The ISL toolchain (`api/isl`) code-generates **Rust** bindings for the whole schema language: the frozen/ABI struct subset (struct/enum/bits/array/handle-ref), **unions** (D66), **tables** (D67), the bounded collections **`string:N`/`vector<T>:N`** (D68), and **protocols** (interface id + method ordinals + request/response/event dispatch, D69). The one remaining wire-codegen gap is **nested `table`/`union` fields** (a table/union field whose type is itself a table or union — the emitter `compile_error!`s these). Also still deferred (non-Rust / tooling): C/C++/Swift/Kotlin bindings, generated fuzzers, trace decoders, redaction codegen, mock servers, and the ABI-diff tool. | Nested-record fields on the stable IR; the other artifacts as their consuming subsystems land. |
| D11 | A protocol's 64-bit interface ID is the first eight little-endian bytes of `SHA-256("fqname@major")`, with `major` fixed at 1. The spec left the algorithm and major-version handling open; this fixes them. | Ratified or revised when version negotiation and the first cross-node protocol ship. |
| D12 | The ISL parser's fuzz coverage is a seeded randomized no-panic test, not a libfuzzer target (the fuzzing toolchain is not yet vendored). | A structure-aware libfuzzer target lands with the fuzzing infrastructure. |
| D13 | ~~SHA-256 is duplicated in `api/isl/src/sha256.rs` and `tools/checks/src/sha256.rs`.~~ (done, D146) Both copies are gone; `//api/hash` is the one implementation, and it is `no_std` and allocation-free rather than host-only, because its third caller measures an image at boot. The interface ids and the vendored-file pins are unchanged, which is what the schema gate and the licence gate check. | ~~Factored into a shared `lib/` hash crate.~~ (`api/`, beside the other two dependency-free rule crates, rather than a `lib/` that does not exist) |
| D14 | **The epoch facility exists (D228)**; the handle table does not yet ride it. Lookup is still a plain read kept free of shared writes, which is correct while the boot CPU is the only CPU that reaches a handle table. | Put the handle table on `kcore::epoch`, once a secondary can reach the executive at all — which needs the machine-half lock. |
| D15 | Object and handle tables (and object refcounts) are fixed-capacity pools with plain-integer counts — there is no general kernel allocator or per-CPU refcount sharding yet. | Dynamic tables and sharded counts when the kernel allocator and SMP land. |
| D16 | The kernel's native `Rights` (`kernel/kcore/src/rights.rs`) and the ISL `handle_abi.isl` `bits Rights` share the Rights Catalog bit values by convention, not by a mechanical cross-check. | The ABI-diff / codegen path enforces agreement. |
| D17 | Synchronous IPC has only the same-core handoff path (a direct caller↔callee `ContextOps::switch`); the cross-core remote path (B24, a per-core wakeup mailbox plus IPI) is not built. The `Executive`/`Scheduler` are already per-CPU, so the remote path is additive. | **Mailbox and IPI: done (D224).** What remains is the IPC path using them — a synchronous call whose callee is on another CPU. Lands with the per-CPU schedulers. |
| D18 | A caller's priority is carried to the callee for a synchronous call's duration (`Thread::priority`) and **selection now honours it** — `Scheduler::pop_ready` takes the highest-priority `Ready` thread, round-robin within a level. What is still missing is *when* that decision is made: a waking high-priority thread waits for the running thread's next preemption point rather than displacing it, and contention outside a synchronous call (a lock's owner) raises nothing. | A wake that outranks the running thread preempts it, and owner-aware locks raise a holder that is not a callee. |
| D19 | Channel messages carry an inline byte payload (≤ 256 B) and a small transferred-handle set (≤ 4) only; ~~out-of-line memory (transfer/share/snapshot)~~ (transfer mode done, D131; share and snapshot still deferred), data-class labels, deadlines, and cancellation are deferred (peer-closed is v0's only unblock-without-reply). Async **ports** now land (D38), but the source bindings (channel readiness, sync point-reached, cancellation, peer-death) and async deadlines that ride on them do not. | Lands with the port source-bindings and cancellation milestone. |
| D20 | B3 (IPC round trip) is now **measured** by the perf rig, with the two-switches-per-round-trip validity check passing at boot; B4 (per transferred handle) and B19 (scaling) still lack benchmarks (D36). Numbers are QEMU correctness/regression only (D34). | B4/B19 benchmarks (D36); R1 compliance (D34). |
| D21 | SYSCALL/SYSRET is single-core (BSP only); the kernel is mapped into every user address space's higher half (`KernelAddressSpace::new_user` copies the kernel PML4 entries), so there is no KPTI / Meltdown isolation and no per-CPU syscall stack beyond the BSP. | SMP bring-up plus the hardening milestone (per-CPU syscall stacks, KPTI). |
| D22 | User↔kernel copies are validated up front against the process mapping table (`syscall::validate_user_range`); the sanctioned fault-capable copy helper with an exception-fixup table (docs/kernel/03) is not built, so a copy never faults — it is rejected before it starts. | Lands with the pager / hardening. |
| D23 | A ring-3 fault uses the default policy — report and terminate the faulting process (`user_fault_handler`); the exception-channel delivery chain (thread→process→job→debugger, docs/kernel/03) is deferred. | Lands with exception channels. |
| D24 | Syscall argument struct layouts were shared between the ISL schema (`handle_abi.isl`/`process_abi.isl`/`channel_msg.isl`), conformance-gated, and a hand-written kernel decoder (`kcore/src/syscall.rs`) by convention (cf. D16); schema-generated in-kernel validation was deferred (the cargo inner loop cannot depend on the bazel-generated bindings). — **CLOSED (D54):** every `decode_*_args` in `kcore/src/syscall.rs` now decodes through its **ISL-generated** `WireDecode` binding (`crate::isl_binding::{process,handle,channel}`); the hand-rolled `le_u32`/`le_u64`/`word32`/`word64` readers are deleted. The schema is the machine-enforced wire-layout source of truth for the whole syscall boundary. | — (done) |
| D54 | **Kernel consumes an ISL-generated binding.** Establishes the schema→binding→`no_std`-kernel-consumer pipeline and migrates the first decoder off hand-rolled byte-poking: `kcore::syscall::decode_process_create_args` now calls `ProcessCreateArgs::decode` (ISL-generated `WireDecode` over the zero-dep `tessera-isl-runtime` codec) and only enforces the semantic header (`size/version/flags/reserved`) on the typed result — no more `le_u32`/`le_u64` for that struct. The **cargo↔bazel bridge** is the crux: kcore's first `build.rs` generates the binding via `tessera_isl::compile`+`codegen_rust::emit` into `OUT_DIR` (honoring D24 — the cargo loop depends on no Bazel output), while Bazel reuses the existing `//api/isl:process_abi_bindings` genrule crate; `kcore/src/lib.rs`'s `mod isl_binding` selects between them on `--cfg=isl_bazel` (Bazel-set), so kcore source is identical for both. Two supporting changes: the ISL codegen now emits `#![no_std]` (so the generated crate links into the bare-metal kernel — cargo strips inner attributes when `include!`ing into the already-`no_std` kcore), and `process_abi_bindings` visibility widened to `//kernel/kcore`. `kcore` gains real deps on `tessera-isl-runtime` (dep) + `tessera-isl` (build-dep). No behavior change (the existing `decode_process_create_args` unit tests + the `process_abi_conformance_test` golden pin it; the `loader:` boot verdict — ProcessCreate → child exits 42 — is byte-identical). No new `unsafe` (`isl-runtime` is `#![deny(unsafe_code)]`). Delivers what D24 deferred ("schema-generated in-kernel validation"; the ABI-diff/codegen path now enforces agreement). **Extended to all six syscall decoders** (`process_create`/`process_start`/`address_space_map` via `process_abi`; `duplicate` via `handle_abi`; `channel_create`/`channel_msg` via `channel_msg`) — the pipeline now generates three schemas into per-schema submodules of `mod isl_binding` (each schema owns a `Rights` bits type, so they cannot be flattened), and the ISL codegen emits `#![no_std]`; the Rights-bearing decoders rely on the generated `Rights`' unknown-bit rejection (every demo/test value is within `KNOWN_BITS`). Fully closes D24. The in-kernel `ipc.rs` `MessageHeader` (an in-memory *subset* of the ISL wire header, not a `decode_*_args` — nothing serializes it) is machine-checked against the generated `channel_msg.MessageHeader` via `to_wire`/`from_wire` + a round-trip test, closing its "shaped to by convention" gap without a synthetic wire path. | Protocol/method dispatch codegen (D10); ~~wire the same pipeline for structured-observability events~~ (done, D57). |
| D25 | The **three-phase create → populate → start** model and an **ELF loader** now land: the kernel loads a real embedded ELF64 root task (parse → map each `PT_LOAD` with W^X → start at `e_entry`), which runs in ring 3, syscalls, and exits clean; jobs landed at M11; the process-lifecycle **syscall ABI** (`ProcessCreate`/`AddressSpaceMap`/`ProcessStart` + `process_abi.isl`) is defined and dispatched. What remains: multiple address spaces per process, and moving the loader/parse into a real user-space loader (D42). The other ring-3 demos keep their asm blobs; only the root task is ELF-loaded. | The user-space loader + component-manager milestone. |
| D26 | B1 (null syscall), B2 (handle op), and B7 (context switch, same- and cross-address-space) are now **measured** by the perf rig at boot. Numbers are QEMU correctness/regression only; R1 compliance pending (D34), and v0's measurement simplifications are D35. | R1 compliance (D34). |
| D27 | The external-pager **page-in** path lands in-kernel (a `Backing::Object` fault → pager `supply` → resume), and **write-back / eviction / software dirty-tracking** now land too (the pager-pressure harness, D41): pages are supplied read-only, a write faults to mark them dirty, clean pages are evicted under a resident cap, and dirty pages are written back (marked clean only after the pager ack) with per-object dirty throttling. What remains: ~~the write-back **reservation** (S3)~~ (done, D55), and moving the pager from a kernel thread into a user-space service (D31). | The user-space-pager milestone. |
| D28 | Lazy demand-fill applies to user anonymous mappings only; kernel mappings remain eagerly populated, so a kernel fault never depends on the fault path faulting in its own stack. | Intentional; revisited with per-CPU fault stacks. |
| D29 | Frame reclaim is a bounded free-list (`MAX_FREE_FRAMES`) plus a bounded shared-frame refcount side-table (`MAX_SHARED_FRAMES`); exceeding either cap leaks the frame but counts the event (`reclaim_overflows`). A general per-frame refcount and unbounded reclaim await the kernel allocator (cf. D15). | With the kernel allocator. |
| D30 | B8 (anonymous zero-fill fault) and B9 (copy-on-write fault) are now **measured** by the perf rig (timing `resolve_fault` directly, D35). Numbers are QEMU correctness/regression only; R1 compliance pending (D34). | R1 compliance (D34). |
| D31 | The pager is a single in-kernel kernel thread and the object→pager binding is implicit (one pager). The user-space pager *service*, the pager syscall surface (create-pager/bind, supply/writeback/evict from ring 3), multiple pagers, an explicit binding table, and cross-core page-in (B24) are deferred. | The user-space-pager milestone. |
| D32 | The page-in path, write-back/eviction/dirty-tracking, and **pager-death → faulted-state + exact data-integrity (lost-dirty-range) event** now land (harness S6, D41). Still deferred: ~~response deadlines + supervision/restart of a hung pager (S7), self-paging cycle detection (S5)~~ (done, D55), and truncate-past-mapped faulting. | The user-space pager (D41). |
| D33 | B10 (external pager page-in) is now **measured under pressure** by the pager-pressure harness (`prototypes/02` S1, D41) — the page-in latency curve at ~50/90/99 % utilization, reported at boot; QEMU numbers are correctness/regression only and R1 compliance is bare-metal (D34). ~~The page-in observability is still a served-count / perf-row rather than the full structured page-in-latency event.~~ (done, D57: `forward_page_in` emits a per-page-in structured latency event.) | R1 compliance (D34); ~~the structured-event pipeline~~ (done, D57). |
| D34 | Budget *compliance* is judged only on bare-metal R1 (prototypes/01: "QEMU/KVM runs validate harness correctness only"); the rig runs in QEMU (TCG in CI), where RDTSC measures emulation time, so its numbers are for harness correctness and regression tracking, not compliance. The R1-hardware gate, both load conditions, and the three-run reproducibility check are pending real hardware. | The bare-metal R1 CI rig lands. |
| D35 | v0 measures a reduced iteration count (~1024 vs the mandated 1M after 10k warm-up); B1 is a ring-3 batch **mean** (not per-sample percentiles); B3/B7 are timed **kernel-thread-to-kernel-thread** (not full user-to-user, awaiting ring-3 channel syscalls) and B8/B9 time `resolve_fault` directly (not the full hardware `#PF`→resume); the invariant-TSC "refuse to run" guard is relaxed to a reported flag. | The full rig on R1 with ring-3 channel syscalls. |
| D36 | The Contended and Scaling load conditions, two-frequency configs, and pinning/environment records are not built, nor the cross-core (B5/B24), interrupt→handler (B32), per-handle (B4), and scaling (B19/B20) benchmarks — they need SMP / interrupt objects / handle-transfer machinery. B6 (wait-on-address) is now measured (D39). Reporting is a serial table plus the ISL result-schema seam (`bench_result.isl`), not the full structured-event pipeline. | As each underlying mechanism lands. |
| D37 | Wait-on-address is keyed by `(space, virtual addr)`, not the physical frame, so there is no cross-process shared-memory futex; the compare-and-block is atomic only by single-core cooperative execution (no per-bucket lock / preempt-disable); there is **no deadline/timeout** though every blocking call should carry one; the wake semantics (wake up to `count`, no bitset, no requeue, no guaranteed FIFO among multiple waiters on one key) are defined here because the design (docs/kernel/04) is silent; and there is no owner-aware-lock / priority-inheritance path (BM-6's boosted-waiter case). Fixed `MAX_WAITERS` pool. | Physical-frame keying, deadlines, and the owner-aware lock land with SMP / the synchronization-hardening milestone. |
| D38 | Ports land as an in-kernel primitive: `Port` with per-`(source, signal)` slot preallocation, coalescing into one event carrying a pending count, drain-reads-current-state, and no overflow (docs/kernel/04). What remains: a **source** is an abstract id, not yet a channel-readiness / sync point-reached / cancellation / peer-death binding; there is no async deadline / expired-flag delivery / drop-policy counter (control-plane only in v0); there is no ring-3 port syscall surface (a single-threaded process cannot drive a reactor, D25); the object/handle/rights bridge is deferred so ports use a raw `PortId` (like channels' raw `EndpointId`, though `ObjectType::Port` exists in the taxonomy); and only one blocked drainer per port (no shared/concurrent receivers) and no cross-core signal+IPI (B5). Fixed `MAX_PORTS`/`MAX_BINDINGS` pools. | The source-binding, reactor / ring-3 port syscall, handle-bridge, and cross-core milestones. |
| D39 | The B6 benchmark measures a kernel-thread `wait`/`wake` round-trip half (÷2), not the ring-3 BM-6 wake-call-entry-to-waiter-running, the owner-aware-lock inheritance-boosted path, or the cross-core B5 signal+IPI. QEMU numbers are harness-correctness / regression only (D34). | The full R1 rig with ring-3 wait/wake and the owner-aware lock. |
| D40 | Jobs land as an in-kernel primitive: a job tree (root + nesting, every member process in exactly one job), kill-the-subtree **innermost-first**, an object-count limit on member processes that **tightens-only** down the tree (the offending create fails with `LimitExceeded`, a resource error), a **state port** (reusing the M10 port primitive) signalling member-exit/emptiness, and rights-checked create/add/kill (`CREATE_JOB`/`CREATE_PROCESS`/`KILL`). Deferred: the separate `resource_domain` object (CPU/memory/IO/accelerator ceilings and the threads/handles/channels/mappings counts); suspend/resume-all-members; default security-context inheritance; the exception-delivery chain (thread→process→job→debugger, `kernel/03`); admission integration (`kernel/07`); ring-3 job syscalls and the object/handle/rights bridge (jobs use a raw `JobId`, like ports/channels, though `ObjectType::Job` is real per job); killing a **running** ring-3 member mid-execution (needs IPI/fault machinery, so v0 terminates member threads that are parked/not-yet-run); the root job is created by the demo, not a boot-time singleton; an empty job is *signalled* on its state port, not auto-destroyed (doc-faithful supervisor reclaim); no reparenting (kill is whole-subtree); cross-core. Fixed `MAX_JOBS`/per-job member & child pools. Job create/kill is unbudgeted (`docs/architecture/03` has no job row). | The resource-domain, exception-channel, admission, ring-3-syscall/handle-bridge, and SMP milestones. |
| D41 | The pager-pressure harness (`docs/prototypes/02`) v0 covers scenarios **S1, S2, S4, S6, S8** with **software** dirty tracking (a page is supplied read-only and its first write faults `WriteToClean` to mark it dirty — no hardware A/D bits, which `karch-x86_64/src/paging.rs` does not even define). Dirty/resident state is per memory object with **one mapping per object** (`kcore::pager::ObjectCache`, not a shared multi-mapping page cache). **S1** times the kernel-side page-in+evict path at ~50/90/99 % frame utilization (a resident cap models utilization; the IPC handoff itself is measured by B3), reduced iterations, QEMU-correctness-only (D34/D35). **Write-back is a synchronous in-kernel operation** (snapshot re-protected read-only → ack → mark clean), not yet an IPC to a separate priority-inherited pager thread; throttling **blocks the writer** at the write fault with a per-object bound (no per-domain bound / attribution). Deferred: ~~the write-back **reservation** / reclaim-deadlock (S3), self-paging **cycle detection** (S5), page-in **deadline + supervision** (S7)~~ (done, D55/M22); the adversarial pager, multi-process/multi-job workloads, the liveness watchdog, the pressure controller / memory ballooning, the pager handle/rights bridge (`SUPPLY`/`WRITEBACK`/`EVICT` bits exist but stay a seam), the user-space pager service, truncate-past-mapped faulting, the B16 flush baseline, and the bare-metal R1 gate. | The adversarial-pager + real-timer watchdog infra, and the user-space pager + bare-metal R1 rig. |
| D42 | **The ring-3 loader gap is closed (M14).** A ring-3 root task now drives `ProcessCreate` → `AddressSpaceMap` → `ProcessStart` to create, populate, and start a **child process** from user space (the docs' user-space-loader model, `docs/api/01`) — the three syscalls are no longer `ENOSYS`. Processes became handle-addressable via `ProcessTable` + a `process_of_id` scan (the `ObjectTable`-payload unification is still deferred). The root-task ELF is still a Bazel-built artifact **embedded via a generated byte array** (v0's "initrd"), not a real initrd / Limine module; still `ET_EXEC` + `PT_LOAD` at fixed addresses (no dynamic linking / relocation / `PT_INTERP` / `ET_DYN` / ASLR, no `PT_TLS`/`PT_GNU_STACK`), and **no signature/measurement** in the load path (the initrd is the trust boundary the docs assume). **Remaining under D42, two of four now closed (composition roadmap Phase 1).** ~~A ring-3 ELF parser~~ — **closed by D249/D251**: `userspace/roottask` is a compiled program whose `parse_elf` reads the header and program table itself and issues one `AddressSpaceMap` per `PT_LOAD`, with **D258** widening it to the ELF32 class. ~~The initial-handle-set / startup-message / bootstrap-channel wire format~~ (only a seeded `create-process` job handle in v0) — **closed by D249, D261 and D253**: `ProcessGrant` installs the handle set a *parent* chose rather than one boot seeded, `kcore::loader::deliver_startup_message` copies the message into a page mapped in the child before its thread exists, and ring-3 `ChannelCreate` retires the boot-installed bootstrap channel D45 describes from the IPC side. **Still open:** shared memory objects and cross-process map of arbitrary memory objects, which are one gap wearing two names — `MemoryMap` maps an object into the caller's *own* space, and `TransferMode::SHARE` is decoded and then refused `NotSupported` until every port carries an object table to refcount against (D19, D131). **Also still open, and it is the whole of composition roadmap Phase 2**: the root-task ELF is a linked byte array and nothing measures it. | The initrd/Limine-module delivery, memory-object loader, dynamic linking, and load-time attestation milestones — the delivery and attestation halves are composition roadmap Phase 2, and the bootstrap-channel install this line used to name is done (D253). |
| D43 | **Kernel self-map GOT coverage (fixed).** `kernel_sections` mapped only `[__requests_start, __requests_end)` of the first (writable) load segment, leaving the linker-placed `.got`/`.got.plt` — which the compiler emits for some indirect/cross-crate references — unmapped once it grew past the first page. After the CR3 switch, an indirect GOT call faulted (surfaced by linking `kcore::elf`, which grew the GOT). Fixed by placing the GOT in the requests segment (`linker.ld`) and mapping the whole first segment to `__text_start`. No remaining gap; noted so the boundary invariant (every allocated section is self-mapped) is explicit. | None (invariant now covered). |
| D44 | **User-space-loader ABI + populate mechanism (M14).** `AddressSpaceMapArgs` was extended 48→56 bytes (append `src: uint64`) — safe because the syscall was `ENOSYS` with no external caller (ABI change). v0 populates a child by **map-then-copy from a validated parent buffer into the child's frames through the HHDM direct map** (`AddressSpace::copy_in` → the port's `write_bytes_to_frame`, no CR3 switch), chosen over the docs' "map memory objects" (no writable shareable memory objects yet). `create-process` is a single seeded-job-handle gate (no real job containment); the child's process/thread run on a **shared scheduler** with a synchronous parent↔child start handoff (mirroring `Executive::call`/`reply`), and a faulting child is handed back to the waiting parent with code −1 (minimal contained-fault handling). The loader parent runs on an oversized **32-page kernel stack** because building a `Process` by value inside the `ProcessCreate` syscall spans ~66 KiB of unoptimized-build stack. **Child exit does not reclaim frames** (bump allocator, no free path) — _superseded by D50 (M20): the loader exit path now reclaims the child's frames, slots, and address space._ One child, one thread, one flat blob; page-aligned `copy_in` destination only. | Memory-object-based populate, real job containment, multi-child/thread, ~~frame reclamation on exit~~ (done, D50), and full contained-fault reporting. |
| D45 | **Ring-3 channel IPC (M15).** The channel transport + `Executive::call`/`reply` synchronous handoff are now reachable from ring 3: a ring-3 **client** `ChannelCall`s a ring-3 **server** (inline bytes + a transferred capability handle) and the server `ChannelReply`s, across two address spaces — the first ring-3↔ring-3 RPC. Syscalls `ChannelCreate`(11)/`ChannelSend`(12)/`ChannelRecv`(13)/`ChannelCall`(14)/`ChannelReply`(15) were added; `ChannelMsgArgs` (72 B) was appended to `channel_msg.isl` (conformance-gated). Handles become channel-addressable via a `Channel.objects[2]` + `endpoint_of_object` bridge (the `process_of_id` pattern). **v0 deviations, four of five now closed.** ~~The channel is created and its endpoints installed by the **boot context before start** (bootstrap-channel model) — ring-3 `ChannelCreate` stays `ENOSYS` because a syscall returns one value, not two endpoint handles (needs a write-back args struct)~~ — **closed by D253**: the write-back args struct is what it took, and the root task now creates its own channels before it has children to give them to. ~~Ring-3 `ChannelSend` (one-way) stays `ENOSYS`, host-tested only~~ — **closed by D150**: the arm was missing rather than the mechanism, and a NIC driver that speaks first is what forced it. ~~Verification is **handler-side** (the kernel never writes reply bytes back into user memory — no writable user page; returning bytes into a user buffer is deferred)~~ — **closed by D79**: `ChannelMsgArgs`'s `inline_ptr`/`inline_len` describe a receive buffer the shared dispatcher copies the payload out into, through `write_user`. ~~One round trip~~ — **closed by D82**: `ChannelReplyRecv` (25) wires `Executive::reply_receive`, and a resident server loops on it. **Still open:** rights reuse `WRITE`/`READ`/`TRANSFER` (no new Rights-Catalog bit — the catalog grew for other facilities, not for channels), and **channel teardown from ring 3**. The second is the one worth naming precisely, because it looks done and is not: `HandleClose` (4) drops an endpoint handle and has branches for memory objects and devices but **none for endpoints**, so it never reaches `Executive::close_endpoint` and never wakes a peer blocked on the other side. That still happens only when the holder exits, through the kernel's own `close_endpoints_of_current`. | Channel teardown from ring 3, which is all that is left of this line: an endpoint branch in `handle_close` that closes the endpoint and wakes the blocked peer, rather than deferring both to process exit. Ring-3 `ChannelCreate`/`ChannelSend` (D253, D150), reply-into-user-buffer (D79) and multi-message `reply_receive` servers (D82) are closed, and the device-manager / driver-host / filesystem services this line said they would unblock all exist. |
| D46 | **Driver-host substrate + real IRQ loop (M16).** A ring-3 **driver host** owns a real device (COM2 16550, IRQ3, internal loopback): its interrupt is delivered as a **port event** (`timer::unmask_irq` + a `trap::DEVICE_IRQ_HOOK` mirroring the timer preempt hook → `Executive::port_signal`), it reads the device register through a **capability-gated port-I/O syscall** (`ObjectType::Device`, offset-checked, compile-time `COM2_BASE`), and it services a **client**'s I/O request over an M15 channel — the driver-host I/O bet (B11 measured, `io-submit`→`driver-visible`). New syscalls `PortCreate`(16)/`PortBind`(17)/`PortWait`(18)/`DeviceIoRead`(19)/`DeviceIoWrite`(20); ports become handle-addressable via a `Port.object` + `port_of_object` bridge (the `process_of_id` pattern). Driver ring-3 threads run **IF-set** via a gated `USER_IF_ON_ENTRY` (not a global `USER_RFLAGS` change — else later demos would take the timer IRQ under the scheduler's `TICK_HOOK`); the M16 demo runs **before `scheduler_demo`**. Reentrancy is safe by construction: SYSCALL's SFMASK + the interrupt gates keep every kernel context IF-clear, so `port_signal` from the IRQ only ever runs while the driver is in ring 3; the driver's IRQ arrives **before** it would park (asserted-before-wait), so no idle-halt-with-interrupts path is needed. **v0 deviations:** MMIO/`map_device` deferred (no non-PCI MMIO device on q35 — the device is port I/O); **single-thread** driver (no request pipelining); the `Device` capability hard-codes COM2's port range (no `ObjectTable` payload — the D42/D45 deferral); the kernel performs COM2 register bring-up (the driver does I/O, not full init); `PortWait` returns a pending count (no `PortEvent` write-back into a user buffer); port ops pass scalars in registers (no ISL wire struct, like the other scalar syscalls); `smoke_boot.sh` gained a second `-serial null` for COM2; **B11 is QEMU/TCG-rig-validation only** (D34/D41), and **B32** (interrupt→driver-host latency) stays deferred (D36). | virtio/PCI MMIO drivers + `map_device`, interrupt objects + B32, two-thread pipelined hosts, the device-capability `(base,len)` payload, ring-3 device bring-up, `PortEvent` write-back, and port teardown/`PortSignal` from ring 3. |
| D47 | **Device manager + resource-graph payload (M17).** A ring-3 **device-manager** service brokers binding: a driver host `ChannelCall`s for its device and the manager **grants a Device capability transferred over the channel reply**. Two backward-compatible wrapper changes enable a granted cap to ride a reply — `chan_channel_reply` now `transfer`s, `chan_channel_call` installs `reply.handles()` (both no-ops for prior demos, whose replies carry no handle). **The ObjectTable-payload deferral (D42/D45/D46) is closed for devices:** an `ObjectType::Device` object now carries a real `(base, len, irq)` **resource-graph payload** in a `DeviceTable` on the `Executive` (the Process/Channel/Port side-table pattern — `ObjectTable::Entry` stays `{type, refcount}`), and `driver_device_io` reads+enforces `(base,len)` from it via generic `device_in/out(base+offset)` instead of the compile-time COM2 constant (M16 steps 3/4/5 register their COM2 node and are the regression test). The kernel `DeviceTable` is the minimal normalized resource graph (node = port range + IRQ line; v0 registers one real node, COM2, from boot). Three ring-3 processes (manager/driver/client, ASIDs 8/9/10) on one executive; the granted cap installs at the driver's runtime handle raw 3; the Device object's reference is conserved manager→message→driver (refcount 1). **v0 deviations:** the IRQ→port bridge stays a kernel constant (`COM2_IRQ_LINE`) — the **interrupt-object grant is still deferred** (B32/D36); the manager holds and `take`s a single pre-seeded cap (no device-id-indexed graph lookup in the ring-3 blob, no `duplicate`-to-grant-many); one real device node (no ACPI/DT/PCI discovery); port I/O only (MMIO deferred, D46). | Interrupt-object grants + B32, device-id-indexed brokering / `duplicate`-to-many, real discovery sources and multiple device nodes, and MMIO device ranges. |
| D48 | **RAM-backed filesystem service (M18).** The external pager's server moves to **ring 3**: a ring-3 client maps a pager-backed memory object, its page fault drives the existing `forward_page_in`→`exec.call` handoff **unchanged** (the only shared-code change is an inert-for-M12 `FS_PENDING` stash), and a ring-3 **FS service** supplies the page from its own RAM buffer via new `PageServe`(21)/`PageSupply`(22) syscalls. The kernel keeps the privileged `supply_page`+frame alloc; the service provides the content — the 4 KiB page (too big for the 256 B inline) is copied from the service's buffer through the **HHDM while the service's CR3 is active** (the M14 discipline), then `supply_page`d into the faulter (page-table edits via the HHDM, no CR3 switch). Client = `USER_PROCESS` (the resolver's faulter), service in `PROCESSES`; both on one `Executive`; service scheduled first (parks in `PageServe`). Completes the roadmap exit-gate "external pager with a RAM-backed filesystem service" (`docs/roadmap/01`); the in-kernel `pager_demo` (M12) stays as the regression test. **v0 deviations:** one pager via the global `PAGER_ENDPOINTS` (per-object pager-endpoint routing deferred); the "file" is a fixed pre-filled buffer (no paths/resize/truncate); write-back/eviction/dirty-tracking stay the in-kernel `ObjectCache` accounting demos S2/S4/S6/S8 (ring-3 write-back deferred); a seeded endpoint handle, not a typed pager object with `supply`/`writeback`/`evict`/`admin` rights; a single-slot `FS_PENDING` (one in-flight fault, synchronous single-core). | Per-object pager routing, ring-3 write-back/eviction, a typed pager object + rights, real filesystem semantics, and concurrent faults. |
| D49 | **Component manager (M19).** A ring-3 **component manager** launches a service (the M14 loader syscalls `ProcessCreate`/`AddressSpaceMap`/`ProcessStart`, unchanged), **supervises** it via the synchronous `ProcessStart`-returns-exit-code handoff, and **restarts** it per a restart policy: a crash-countdown retries a "crashing" service (non-zero exit) until it comes up clean, and a **restart budget** hard-caps the retries (a service that keeps crashing is given up on — the manager exits a distinct `CM_GIVEUP_CODE`, never running away toward the leak bound). Reuses `loader_syscall_handler`/`LOADER_SCHED`/`PROCESSES` and the arg decoders **wholesale**; the manager is an in-kernel `global_asm!` blob + a writable data page (the `wait_demo` pattern) that patches the returned child handle (rax) and the policy `arg` into the loader arg structs at run time (absolute VAs — a bare symbol assembles as a memory ref). The one shared-code change was a **per-launch child-kstack stride** in `loader_process_start` (`CHILD_KSTACK_BASE + CM_LAUNCHES*CHILD_KSTACK_STRIDE`) so restarts don't collide in the shared `kernel_vm` — _retired by D50 (M20): the kstack is reclaimed on exit and a single fixed window is reused._ Closes the roadmap's "Service dependency restart" gate (`docs/architecture/01`); the recovery path (countdown 3 → 4 real runs, exit codes 3,2,1,0, manager clean) and the budget-cap self-test (countdown 10, budget 4 → capped at 4 runs, give-up code) are the boot checks, with `runs == launches` proving no restart collided. **v0 deviations:** **no reclaim on exit** — each launch leaks a process slot (`MAX_PROCESSES=16`), a scheduler thread slot (left Blocked), and frames, so restart is bounded (~15×; a real supervisor needs the deferred teardown/reclaim built on `job_kill`) — _superseded by D50 (M20): reclaim-on-exit lifts the bound (41-restart boot check, frames/slots back to baseline);_ **synchronous single-core** supervision — the manager blocks in `ProcessStart` while the service runs, one service at a time (async job-state-port supervision needs a process→job back-link + emit-signal-on-exit + a ring-3 job-source bridge, all deferred); the crash is **simulated** via the service's exit code (no real fault-crash loop); all restarted children share `LOADER_CHILD_ASID=3` (per-child ASID deferred, harmless single-core); the manager is an **in-kernel blob** (a signed-ELF service + a real component manifest are deferred). | ~~Reclaim-on-exit (process/thread/frame teardown)~~ (done, D50), async job/port supervision, per-child ASID allocation, real crash detection, and a real component manifest / signed-ELF service. |
| D50 | **Reclaim-on-exit — unbounded service restart (M20).** Lifts D49/D44's "no reclaim on exit": when a launched child exits (or faults) and hands back to its supervisor, `loader_process_start` now returns **all** of its resources to their pools — the scheduler **thread slot** (`Scheduler::reap`) and its **kernel-stack frames** in the shared `kernel_vm` (`AddressSpace::reclaim_range`), the **process-table slot** (`ProcessTable::remove`) and the child's **address space** — leaf frames + the page-table frames it uniquely owns (`AddressSpace::teardown` → the new arch `AddressSpaceOps::free_tables`, which walks only the user-owned lower half `[0, 256)` of the PML4 plus the root, leaving the shared kernel tables `new_user` copied in untouched) — and the parent's **handle** to the child (`HandleTable::close`, releasing the object slot). Leaf reclaim reuses the `evict_page` unmap-then-`free_frame` path; the frame allocator's bounded free-list already existed (`free_frame`/`reclaim_overflows`). All new kcore primitives are host-tested (teardown/reclaim_range return the free-list to baseline; reap frees the slot and refuses the running thread; a reaped index is never dispatched — `RunQueue::remove` + a `pop_ready` `None`-skip). The **safe ordering** is the key: the child is the current thread on its own kstack when it exits, so reclaim runs only **after** `handoff_to(parent)` returns — the child is off-CPU (parent CR3 active) and its kstack window (edited via the direct map, not the active CR3) is free to unmap. With the kstack reclaimed the M19 per-launch stride is retired for a **single fixed window**, and `CM_LAUNCHES` becomes pure observability. Proof: `cm_reclaim_stress` restarts a service **41 times** (past the old ~15 bound) and comes up clean — impossible without slot reclaim (`MAX_PROCESSES`/`MAX_THREADS = 16`) — drawing only ~46 frames total (bounded, not ∝ 41) with zero reclaim-overflows. Closes `docs/kernel/05` deterministic-reclaim for the single-process case. **v0 deviations:** reclaim is driven **ad hoc from `loader_process_start`**, not from a real **job-subtree teardown built on `job_kill`** (docs/kernel/05) with async **state-port** emit-on-exit supervision; the object/handle tables still bound restart at their (much larger) caps; per-child ASID is still shared (`LOADER_CHILD_ASID=3`); **single-core only** — SMP would need a **TLB shootdown** of the kstack window (currently `invlpg`-only); the crash is still **simulated** via exit code — _real crash detection lands in D51 (M21)_; a spawn failure **mid-launch** (before the child runs) still leaks the partially-populated child object (not exercised by the demo). | Job-subtree teardown + async job/port supervision, object/handle-table reclaim to the same bound, per-child ASID, SMP TLB shootdown, ~~real crash detection~~ (done, D51), and partial-launch rollback. |
| D51 | **Driver-host restart on crash (M21).** A ring-3 **driver host** (the M16 service driver — owns COM2 via a Device capability, takes IRQ3 as a port event, services a client over a channel) **crashes via a real CPU fault** (a null-deref → #PF, vector 14, gated by a crash countdown passed in the ring-3 entry `arg`/rdi). `driver_fault_handler` — `user_fault_handler` rewritten against the **EXEC substrate** (`EXEC`/`PROCESSES`, resolving the faulter via `chan_current_index()`) — contains it (D23 default policy: mark `Exited(-1)`, `terminate` the thread, `yield_to_boot`), returning control to a **kernel-driven supervisor** looping around `exec_ref().run()`. The supervisor reclaims the crashed host with the M20 primitives adapted to EXEC (`reclaim_crashed_driver_host`: `reap` + `reclaim_range` the kstack, `remove` + `teardown` the address space, `release` the process object — but **never** `close` the device handle), **revokes** its device binding (implicit on `Process` teardown — no Drop impl, so the handle is forgotten *without* `release`, refcount-neutral — plus `mask_irq`), and **rebinds** `dev_obj` into a fresh host (`install`, so the shared Device object stays **rc=1** across every restart), **restarting** per a `(countdown, budget)` policy until the host comes up clean and completes the M16 client round trip (byte 0x5a), capping a persistently-crashing host at `DRIVER_RESTART_GIVEUP_CODE`. The Device object + its `DeviceTable` node are created **once** before the loop so they outlive every host. Closes the Stage-0 exit gate *"kill-a-driver-host-under-load recovers per the failure model"* (`docs/roadmap/01`) and the Crash-Recovery ladder (`docs/drivers/01`, "revoke mappings/interrupts → mark degraded → restart host → restore binding"); delivers the **real crash detection** D50 deferred. Boot checks: an isolated fault+reclaim self-test, the recovery demo (2 real #PF crashes → each reclaimed + rebound → clean serve, cap conserved rc=1, no reclaim overflow), and a budget self-test (a host that keeps crashing is capped at 4 launches then given up). All in `main.rs` reusing M16's driver path + M20's reclaim; **no kcore change**. **v0 deviations:** the supervisor is **in-kernel** (a ring-3 driver-supervisor service, `docs/architecture/01` "Driver supervisor", deferred — mirrors M16/M17 kernel-driven bring-up); the crash is a null-deref, not a real **crash-dump / trace-tail capture** (ladder step 3); **device "reset" is `mask_irq` only**, not a hardware reset (step 5); no dependent-service notification (step 4); no **watchdog / escalation** to rollback / fallback-driver / device-quarantine (`docs/drivers/04`, Stage-2); no DMA-revoke; **single host**, **single-core** (SMP kstack shootdown per D50). | Ring-3 driver-supervisor service; ~~crash-dump/trace-tail capture~~ (done, D128); ~~hardware device reset~~ (done, D128); ~~dependent-service notify~~ (done, D128); watchdog escalation — ~~quarantine~~ (done, D128), with rollback still unbuilt and a fallback driver declared and host-tested but not exercised, because this tree has one driver image per class; ~~DMA-revoke~~ (done, D123 when the capability departs and D127 when the device itself faults); and multi-host supervision. |
| D52 | **Substrate consolidation — one scheduler, one dispatcher (refactor).** Retires the parallel **loader substrate** that had accreted alongside the **executive substrate**: `main.rs` carried a standalone `static mut LOADER_SCHED: Scheduler` + `loader_current_index()` + a `loader_syscall_handler` for the M14/M19/M20 loader/component-manager path, while M15–M18/M21 ran on the global `EXEC: Executive` (which *owns* a `Scheduler` plus channels/waits/ports/jobs/devices) via `chan_current_index()` + `channel_syscall_handler`/`driver_syscall_handler` — two notions of "the current process" and three near-identical dispatchers with copy-pasted `Null`/`DebugWrite`/`ProcessExit` arms over the **already-shared** `PROCESSES`/`OBJECTS` tables. The loader's synchronous parent-waits-for-child `ProcessStart` handoff is built entirely from plain `Scheduler` methods (`handoff_to`/`add_thread`/`reap`/`yield_to_boot`/`current`) that `Executive::scheduler()` surfaces unchanged, so it migrates onto `EXEC` verbatim: **(1)** `loader_demo`/`cm_run` init the global `EXEC` (a fresh `Executive` has empty channel/port/job/device tables — inert for the loader path) and drive `exec_ref().run()`; `loader_current_index` is deleted for `chan_current_index`; `loader_process_start`/`_fault_handler` + the M20 reclaim block re-borrow `exec_ref().scheduler()` per op. **(2)** The `PARENT_WAITER` handback branch folds into `chan_process_exit`, so **one** `ProcessExit` path serves both the channel-client (park + `block_current`) and the loader/CM parent (hand back the child's exit code); `loader_process_exit` is deleted. **(3)** The three op-handlers merge into a single `syscall_handler` (all arms: `Null`/`DebugWrite`/`ProcessExit` + `ProcessCreate`/`AddressSpaceMap`/`ProcessStart` + `Channel*`/`Port*`/`DeviceIo*`), installed by every EXEC-substrate demo; ops a demo never issues never fire. Each arm borrows `PROCESSES`/`OBJECTS` **locally** (never handler-wide) so the channel/port ops' internal re-borrow and a child's re-entrant `ProcessExit` during the start handoff never add a *within-call* alias (single-core cooperative — the `exec_ref()` SAFETY invariant). **No behavior change** — verified byte-identical across all 25 boot demos (the deterministic verdict lines diffed clean at each step; only the KASLR direct-map base and ±1 memmap frame count vary boot-to-boot). **No kcore change.** Removes a standing class of bugs (two schedulers drifting out of sync) and lowers the cost of future supervision milestones. Supersedes the implicit dual-substrate arrangement of D42/D44/D45/D46/D49. | Rename the residual `LOADER_`/`CM_`/`M19_` legacy identifiers to descriptive stems; promote the kernel-driven supervisor loop to a ring-3 service (D49/D51). |
| D53 | **Kstack-window + ASID allocators — retire hand-assigned demo resources (refactor).** Every ring-3/kernel demo thread mapped its kernel stack into the shared boot `kernel_vm` at a **hand-picked `0xffff_c000_XXXX_0000` window constant** (33 of them) and tagged its address space with a **hand-numbered `Asid(n)`** (values 0–21). Two windows overlapping panics the boot `AlreadyMapped` (bit twice: the M15 silent jobs regression, M21 vs M18 FS stacks), every new demo paid a "grep all of `0xffff_c000_` before choosing" tax, and a latent alias sat live in-tree (`GUARD_TEST`/`WAIT_WAITER` both `…9000_0000`, safe only because the two demos never coexist). Replaced by two monotonic `main.rs` allocators: `alloc_kstack(pages)` hands out unique 2-MiB windows from a region based at `0xffff_c008_0000_0000` (well above every former const; `map_anonymous` imposes no upper bound and the arch mapper builds intermediate tables lazily), the stack mapping at the slot base with the ~1.9-MiB slack as an unmapped guard gap; `alloc_asid()` is a monotonic `AtomicU16` (the tag is opaque — never a hardware PCID, `.asid()` has no callers — so a never-reused counter is correct). Strided demos (workers, perf, jobs) draw per-thread (perf/jobs reserve a stable per-slot block via `reserve_kstack_block`). The **two restart-loop resources** (loader/component-manager child; restartable driver host) memoize one window+ASID on first launch and reuse it every relaunch — the exited instance's window is freed by `reclaim_range`, the next launch re-maps the same VA — preserving the single-fixed-window reuse M20/M21 rely on (a fresh allocation per relaunch would re-leak VAs). **No behavior change** — verdicts byte-identical across all boot demos at each step, `cm-reclaim` still 41 restarts / 46 frames bounded, `driver-restart` still rebinds rc=1 across 2 crashes. **No kcore change** (demo-harness scaffolding — a real kernel allocates thread stacks per-process via VMA/frame machinery, not one shared window pool). Grep gate after migration: only `KERNEL_VMAP_BASE`, the non-stack `FS_SELFTEST_VA` data page, and `Asid(0)` (boot `kernel_vm`) remain hand-picked. Retires the per-demo window/ASID packing notes of D46/D47/D48/D50 and the `kstack-vmap-slot-collisions` "grep first" rule. | Move the allocators into a host-tested `kcore`/`lib` module if the scheme outgrows the demo harness; a reuse-on-free ASID allocator if PCID tagging is introduced. |
| D55 | **Pager-pressure scenarios S3, S5, S7 (M22).** Closes the last three pager-pressure/self-paging scenarios (`docs/prototypes/02`), delivering the three kernel rules `docs/kernel/03` mandates as real, host-tested `kcore::pager` mechanisms driven by boot demos (the S2/S4/S6/S8 in-kernel accounting-model pattern): **S3 `WriteBackReservation`** — a write-back reservation (L126-132) that keeps the write-back path progressing at hard memory pressure (ordinary allocation blocks cleanly, reserved write-back drains a dirty page so reclaim advances) and fails cleanly (range faulted) when a write-back over-allocates past its reservation; **S5 `SelfPagingGraph`** — the anti-deadlock guard (L112-117) that detects self-paging cycles (pager A↔B mutual backing, and the degenerate single self-pager) in the in-flight waits-for graph and breaks them by faulting the request rather than blocking; **S7 `PageInSupervisor`** — the page-in deadline + supervision rule (L78-83) where a request past its (logical) deadline delivers a bounded fault error (range faulted, never indefinite blocking) and repeated misses escalate to supervised restart, each miss and escalation a counted event. The forbidden outcome in all three is a hang; the demos are deterministic (pressure and deadlines are explicit budgets, no real allocator exhaustion or timer). Satisfies the `docs/roadmap/01` exit-gate clause *"pager pressure and self-paging tests pass"* (the sibling driver clause closed by D51/M21). No new `unsafe` (pure accounting types + host tests). **v0 deviations (stay deferred, D41):** the real ring-3 adversarial pager, a real timer/liveness watchdog (deadlines are a logical tick budget — D37), the pressure controller/ballooning, multi-process workloads, per-object pager routing, the user-space pager service, and the bare-metal R1 rig. | Adversarial-pager + real-timer watchdog infra; user-space pager (D31); bare-metal R1 rig (D34). |
| D56 | **Stage-0 perf gate: QEMU-certified now, bare-metal R1 deferred to hardware-CI (decision).** Records the explicit close-out decision for the exit-gate clause "budgets B1–B11 met on reference class R1" (`docs/roadmap/01`). The perf clause is evaluated in **two tiers**: (1) the in-repo rig (`perf_harness`, D9/D26/D30/D33) runs B1–B11 under QEMU/TCG as a **regression** gate — budgets are tracked continuously and no regression is tolerated (this tier is **met**); (2) **bare-metal R1 compliance** — RDTSC on invariant-TSC reference hardware, full iteration counts, both load conditions, and the three-run reproducibility check (D34/D35) — is a distinct **hardware-CI** gate that requires reference hardware absent from this development environment (prototypes/01: "QEMU/KVM runs validate harness correctness only"). Decision: R1 compliance does **not** block in-repo Stage-0 progress; it is formally scoped to a dedicated hardware-bring-up milestone and is the **one remaining** Stage-0 exit criterion (the sibling clauses closed by D51/M21 driver-host recovery and D55/M22 pager-pressure/self-paging). No code change — the QEMU rig and its numbers are unchanged; this entry formalizes how the clause is satisfied (QEMU regression) vs. deferred (R1 hardware). Supersedes the open framing of D34's "what remains" for the *gate-blocking* question while leaving D34/D35's technical deferrals intact. | The bare-metal R1 CI rig + hardware bring-up (D34); until then Stage-0 is QEMU-certified for perf. |
| D57 | **Structured, ISL-declared observability events (M23).** Closes the second of the two hard coding-guideline gaps the architecture review found (the first, ISL-by-convention serialization, closed by D24/D54): the kernel had *no* structured observability — 190 `kprintln!` sites and ad-hoc counters — against `docs/lifecycle/04` ("Events and tracepoints are ISL-declared; `println!`-style debugging does not land"; "a PR adding a mechanism without its structured events is **incomplete**") and `console.rs`'s standing IOU. Delivers the **emit half**: (1) an ISL event schema (`api/isl/examples/kernel_event.isl`, `library tessera.kernel.observability`) — since ISL has no `event` construct, an event is the ABI-struct shape every kernel record uses: `strict enum` discriminators (`EventKind`/`Severity`/`Component`/`Classification`) plus a 104-byte `@abi struct KernelEvent` carrying the mandated envelope (`docs/observability/01`: timestamp, component, thread, process, severity, event name, schema version, 128-bit correlation id, data classification) and a fixed `arg0..arg3` payload interpreted per kind (unions are not emittable, D10); generated + golden-conformance-checked and consumed by the kernel over the D54 pipeline. (2) `kcore::event` — a bounded `EventRing` (pure, host-tested) behind a global `SpinLock` sink in the `console.rs` shape: emission never blocks and never overwrites an unread record; a full ring **drops at the source, counts, and the next emission with room prefixes one `EVENTS_DROPPED` meta-event** carrying the accumulated count, so a chatty component silences itself but "the silencing is visible" (`docs/observability/02`, "Flood control"; `docs/lifecycle/04`, "No Silent Fallback"). (3) Real emission from the mechanisms that already computed the fields and whose docs already called them events: `PageInSupervisor::record_miss` → deadline-miss + supervision-escalate, `ObjectCache::fault` → the object-faulted **data-integrity record** (the S6 verdict claimed one that did not exist), both `BumpFrameAllocator` reclaim-overflow sites (distinct reasons), and `forward_page_in` → the per-page-in **structured page-in-latency event**. Boot check (`events: OK`) drains the ring, round-trips every emitted record through its ISL binding, and proves the bound. **Closes D33** ("the structured page-in-latency event") and **D54's** "wire the same pipeline for structured-observability events"; advances D36 (bench reporting), D55 (S3/S5/S7 counted → emitted), D51 (crash/restart events), D32 (the data-integrity event now has a schema). Additive — all 28 prior boot verdicts byte-identical. No new `unsafe`. **v0 deviations:** the **severity ladder is the project's choice** (DEBUG/INFO/NOTICE/WARNING/ERROR/CRITICAL, gapped by ten) because `docs/observability/01` mandates the field but never enumerates levels; a **single global ring, not per-CPU** (single core, D8 — the harvest model is not foreclosed); ~~no correlation-id propagation~~ (closed by D59); timestamp is the boot TSC via a clock hook, not the suspend-crossing boot clock; **fixed scalar payload** instead of per-kind structs (D10); `classification` is `PUBLIC` for every v0 event and **redaction codegen is deferred** (D10); no user/tenant id; **no compile-time tracepoint gating, so B31 (2 ns disabled tracepoint) is unmet**; no panic-surviving ring, no flight recorder, and no log/diagnostics service or harvest (Stage 1, `docs/roadmap/01`); the legacy `kprintln!` verdicts are **not yet rendered from events** (the "text is a rendering of the record" migration is the follow-on). | ~~Render the boot verdicts from events~~ (done, D58); ~~correlation-id propagation + fan-out link events~~ (done, D59); per-CPU rings + harvest by a log service (Stage 1); compile-time gating to meet B31; **per-kind event payloads now that union codegen lands (D66)** — the fixed `arg0..arg3` payload was chosen only because unions were not emittable; redaction codegen + trace decoders (D10); panic-surviving ring and flight recorder. |
| D58 | **Boot verdicts rendered from structured records (M24).** Completes the rule D57 left half-done — `docs/observability/01`: *"Plain text rendering is generated from structured records."* Each of the 28 boot demos now builds an ISL-declared `DemoVerdict` (`api/isl/examples/demo_verdict.isl`, `library tessera.kernel.harness`: a `strict enum DemoId` + `Outcome` and an 88-byte `@abi` struct with the mandated envelope and **eight** payload slots) and calls the harness renderer, which produces the verdict line **from the record**. The record is primary; the text is a view. Generated and golden-conformance-checked, consumed over the D54 pipeline and re-exported through `kcore::verdict`. **The consequential half is the exit gate:** the harness had *no* aggregate failure flag — every verdict site was a bare `if pass { OK } else { FAIL }` with no side effect and `kernel_main` ended in an unconditional `ExitCode::Success`, while CI (`tools/qemu/smoke_boot.sh`) only checks the alive marker and status 33. **A boot where all 28 demos printed FAIL still exited 33 and passed the smoke test.** `Outcome::Fail` now increments `DEMOS_FAILED` and the boot exits `ExitCode::Failure`, so a regression fails the build. Proven both ways: the normal boot exits 33 with all 28 verdict lines byte-identical, and a deliberately falsified predicate produced `S8 dirty-query: FAIL`, `TESSERA-STAGE0: 1 demo(s) FAILED`, and **exit 65** (reverted before landing). No new `unsafe`. **v0 deviations:** only the **pass** path is rendered from the record — a failing demo keeps its existing direct `kprintln!` diagnostic, because those strings dump the whole predicate (up to **10** values, plus `Option`/bool) and rendering them would force a wider payload and encodings for non-scalars; verdict args are positional `u64` interpreted per demo by the renderer (signed values carried as `u64` and cast back), not per-demo typed structs (unions are not emittable, D10); the records are rendered immediately and not retained in a ring or harvested (no log service — Stage 1). | Render the FAIL diagnostics from records too (needs a 10-slot payload); retain verdicts for a harvesting consumer; per-demo typed payloads now that union codegen lands (D66) — the blocker is removed; the rewire (a wider record) is the remaining work. |
| D59 | **Correlation-id propagation (M25).** Closes the causal-anonymity hole D57 left and named: events were timestamped and typed but carried `correlation_lo/hi = 0` and `thread_id/process_id = 0`, so nothing joined a page-in to the fault that caused it or a driver-host crash to the restart that followed. Implements `docs/observability/02` ("Correlation IDs, Normatively") for the causality the kernel can see. (1) **`kcore::trace`** — a new module owning the id and the ambient "what is running now" context, deliberately independent of the event ring so `sched`/`exec` carry identity without pulling observability into the switch path. The 128-bit id is `(per-boot epoch, monotonic sequence)`; splitting it that way is load-bearing, not cosmetic — the epoch is written once at boot, so publishing the current id is a **single `AtomicU64` store**, which cannot tear and takes no lock. A `SpinLock<u128>` would deadlock outright, since the timer tick reaches the publish through `on_tick` → `switch_to`. (2) **Thread carriage** — `Thread::correlation` beside `priority`, which the design already describes with the same words ("carried to a callee for a synchronous call's duration"). (3) **One republish point**: `Scheduler::switch_to`'s `self.current = next` is the *only* assignment to `current`, so a single `trace::set_current` there covers `run`/`block_current`/`handoff_to`/`yield_to_boot`/both `on_tick` paths. (4) **Sync propagation**: `Executive::call` carries the caller's id to the callee beside the existing priority carriage and **restores the callee's own** from a `saved_correlation[]` array parallel to `sync_depth` — without the restore a server keeps its last caller's id and misattributes everything it does afterwards. (5) **Fan-out links, not shared ids**: `Scheduler::add_thread` mints a *fresh* id per branch and emits a `CORRELATION_LINK` event naming the parent, so traces form a tree. (6) **Causal origins**: boot (which also installs the epoch) and the COM2 device IRQ — the two points in Stage 0 where "the outside world becomes work". (7) **Exception reports**: the three ring-3 fault handlers previously emitted *no* event at all; they now emit `USER_FAULT_CONTAINED` (vector + faulting address) stamped with the faulting thread's id, giving `docs/kernel/03`'s "report ... and a correlation ID" something to attach to. Verified on target by the new `correlation` demo against the events the *preceding* demos actually emitted: 86 records with a live id and thread identity, the caller's id observed inside the callee mid-call and the callee's own restored after, 49 link events naming a parent distinct from their own fresh id, 7 contained faults carrying the faulting thread's id. The D58 exit gate proved itself en route — an intermediate build where fault events were being dropped printed `correlation: FAIL` and **exited 65**. No new `unsafe`. **`EVENT_RING_CAPACITY` 64 → 256**, because link events made the old bound bite: the restart-supervision demos spawn dozens of threads, the ring filled with links, and — a full ring dropping the *newest* — the fault reports after them were silently discarded. **v0 deviations:** ~~**async messages cannot propagate**~~ (closed by D60) — `docs/observability/02` said the id rides "the header field that already exists", but that field was **counterfactual** (`MessageHeader` has `interface_id`/`method_id`/`flags`/`txn_id`, and `txn_id` is a 64-bit call-matching token, not a causal id), so carrying it would be an ABI change to `channel_msg.isl` + `ChannelMsgArgs` + `ipc.rs`; consequently `call`'s "callee not yet parked" arm cannot stamp a callee that has no index yet, and the pager page-in request still carries no id (`docs/kernel/03`'s "the request carries ... a correlation ID" unmet). Ids are a **monotonic counter, not random** — the design requires 128 bits and freshness but never unpredictability, and its VM-boundary clause is explicit that no host semantics depend on an id; there is no kernel CSPRNG to use and `hw_random` is RDRAND-gated and absent on the CI CPU model (D6). The epoch is **TSC-seeded** for cross-boot distinctness only. The **preemption tick is not an origin** (the design says a timer *program* firing; a per-tick mint would flood) and **syscall entry is not an origin** (the calling thread already carries an id and the syscall inherits it), so `docs/api/01`'s per-syscall trace record is not yet emitted. The ambient context can be **briefly stale** if a tick lands between the publish and the register switch — the same exposure `Scheduler::current` already has, documented in the module header; never torn, and nothing orders on it. `ExceptionSlot` remains unpopulated (the report is an event, not a per-thread slot). | ~~Correlation on the wire (`channel_msg.isl` header + pager page-in request), which also closes the async-send and unparked-callee gaps~~ (done, D60); per-syscall trace records (`docs/api/01`); ids from the kernel CSPRNG once one exists; per-CPU rings + harvest by a log service (Stage 1). |
| D60 | **Correlation on the wire (M26).** Closes the three gaps D59 left, all one root cause — a message carried no causal id, so causality died at every message boundary: async `send` could not propagate (no handoff to carry it), `call`'s unparked-callee arm could not stamp (no callee index yet), and the pager page-in request carried no id (`docs/kernel/03`'s "the request carries ... a correlation ID" unmet). **Makes a counterfactual design sentence true:** `docs/observability/02` said async messages carry the id "in the header field that already exists" — D59 established that field did **not** exist (`MessageHeader` had `interface_id`/`method_id`/`flags`/`txn_id`, and `txn_id` is a call-matching token, not a causal id). Appends `correlation_lo`/`correlation_hi` to `@abi struct MessageHeader` in `channel_msg.isl` (**40 → 56 bytes**, `MESSAGE_HEADER_WIRE_VERSION` 1 → 2), matching `kernel_event.isl`'s split — `_hi` the per-boot epoch, `_lo` the origin-minted sequence. In-memory `ipc::MessageHeader` carries only the sequence and `to_wire` supplies the epoch from `trace::epoch()`, the same split `event::record` uses: the epoch is a boot constant and storing it in all ~1024 queued-message slots would buy nothing. `Message::set_correlation` mirrors the existing `set_txn`. The executive stamps on `call` (beside the txn stamp) and on `send`, and **adopts on a successful dequeue** in `receive` and `reply_receive` — only on success, so a parked retry cannot churn a server's id; a message with no recorded cause (id 0) leaves the receiver's id alone rather than erasing it. **The kernel stamps; a sender never supplies.** `ChannelMsgArgs` — the struct by which ring 3 *describes* a message — is deliberately **unchanged at 72 bytes** and gains no correlation field, for two reasons: semantically, identity must come from kernel-attested context and never payload fields (`docs/lifecycle/04`; the design's VM-boundary clause treats any externally-supplied id as an untrusted annotation), so a ring-3 process must not be able to forge a cause; and practically, nine ring-3 `global_asm!` `.rodata` blobs hand-build that struct starting `.long 72` against an exact size check, so widening it would have broken the chan, driver-host, device-manager, and driver-restart demos at once. `chan_build_message` stamps the calling thread's ambient id instead. The **pager payload also needed no widening**: the page-in request *is* a `Message`, so the header satisfies `docs/kernel/03` directly — the 20-byte inline layout, both its in-kernel parsers, and the ring-3 FS service (a blob that only ever receives a `u64` offset in `rax`, never the `Message`) are untouched. Verified on target by the extended `correlation` demo: every page-in the in-kernel pager served arrived carrying the exact cause its faulting thread sent it with (counted at serve time, not compared at the end — the boot's last page-in is served by the ring-3 FS path, which never reaches `serve_page_request`, so comparing final values compares two different requests). The D58 exit gate caught that mistake in development: the first version printed `correlation: FAIL` with `faulter=0x23 served=0xb` and **exited 65**. Host tests cover the async path the harness has no traffic for (the kernel harness uses only `call`/`reply`, never `send`). Also corrects the `docs/observability/02` sentence to state the kernel-stamps rule. No new `unsafe`; all prior verdicts byte-identical. **v0 deviations:** `from_wire` **drops `correlation_hi`**, so a header from a different boot epoch would lose that distinction — irrelevant while a message cannot outlive the kernel that queued it, but it means the in-memory header is a 64-bit view of a 128-bit wire field. The generated decoder is **canonical, not size-driven** (`isl-runtime`'s `decode` must consume every byte), so although `docs/api/01` "Monotonic Extension" sanctions *"add optional trailing fields"*, in practice this was a **lockstep version bump** — a v1 producer is rejected on size, and true optional-trailing-field decoding needs size-driven codegen (D10 family). The async adopt has **no restore bracket** (unlike the synchronous `call`, which parks and restores the callee's own id): an async server carries the last request's id until its next receive adopts the next one — the design's "pipeline stages inherit the item's ID", but it means a server's *own* background work between requests is attributed to its last caller. Ring-3 pagers still cannot *see* the id (surfacing it would change the `PageServe` syscall return convention). | Restore bracket (or an explicit "own work" id) for async servers; surface the id to ring-3 pagers/servers; size-driven optional-trailing-field decoding so the schema's monotonic-extension rule holds in practice; per-syscall trace records (`docs/api/01`). |
| D61 | **The AArch64 port boots to EL1 (M27).** The second architecture port, and so the first evidence that `tessera-karch` is an abstraction rather than a description of x86-64. New crates: `karch-aarch64` (PL011 console, `CpuOps`/`InterruptControl`, four-level 4 KiB paging with `MAIR`/`TCR`/W^X, AAPCS64 context switch, `VBAR_EL1` exception vectors, generic-timer tick, GICv2, semihosting exit) and `kernel-aarch64` (boot glue: an arm64 `Image` header, EL2→EL1 normalization, a DTB reader, MMU bring-up). Scope is **EL1 only** — no EL0, no `svc`, no ELF loading, no ring-3 — so the seventeen ring-3 `global_asm!` demo blobs are untouched. `karch`/`kcore` were already generic over the porting traits by type parameter, so the second port cost **no `kcore` fork**: the arch-independent core is selected by naming `KernelAddressSpace`/`ContextSwitch` in the binary, exactly as x86-64 does. What the port validated: the trait layer held (`kcore` had only four x86-isms, all trivial — D64); `ContextOps::prepare_resume` already documented `space_root` as "a CR3/**TTBR**-class root", i.e. built for this. **Boot artifact:** a flat arm64 `Image`, produced by a second `--oformat=binary` link of the same objects, not by `objcopy` — host binutils is single-target and cannot read an AArch64 ELF, and `qemu-system-aarch64 -kernel` handed a bare ELF starts with every register zero and **no device tree at all** (the Image header selects the protocol that supplies one). **Exit status:** Arm semihosting `SYS_EXIT`, propagating the same 33/65 the x86-64 harness keys on — PSCI `SYSTEM_OFF` was rejected because it exits 0, colliding with "QEMU ran fine". **v0 deviations:** the kernel runs entirely out of `TTBR0_EL1` with `TTBR1` walks disabled — the kernel-in-`TTBR1`/user-in-`TTBR0` split is *better* than x86-64's shared-PML4 (D21) but needs a high-half link address and a position-independent entry path, which only EL0 needs; **single-core** (no PSCI secondary start, GICv2 not GICv3, `MPIDR.Aff0` as the dense CPU id); **no FP/SIMD save** (softfloat kernel, EL1 FP access left trapping); the DTB is boot-reclaimable but stays reserved (nothing consumes discovery from it yet); no UEFI/Limine path (`-kernel` direct boot is the whole boot chain). | EL0 (the `svc` boundary, lower-EL vectors, `TTBR0`/`TTBR1` split, an AArch64 roottask); SMP + PSCI + GICv3; FP context save; the UEFI/Limine boot chain (D2 family). |
| D62 | **`sync_instruction_cache` is now an undefaulted porting-layer primitive (M27).** `docs/kernel/03` forbids baking x86-64's coherent instruction cache into the ABI, but nothing had required the operation because on x86-64 the assumption is true. It is now a required `AddressSpaceOps` method with **no default body**: a default no-op would be correct on x86-64 and silently wrong everywhere else, the exact bug class the porting layer exists to prevent. x86-64 implements it as a documented no-op; AArch64 does `DC CVAU`/`DSB`/`IC IVAU`/`DSB`/`ISB`, stepping by the line sizes `CTR_EL0` reports. **QEMU-only caveat (measured):** with the AArch64 implementation stubbed to nothing the conformance case still passes under TCG, which invalidates translation blocks on any guest write and so models a coherent icache whatever the target — so the case proves the *plumbing* under emulation and can only *fail* on real hardware; same QEMU-certified-only tier as the perf budgets (D34/D56). | Bare-metal hardware CI, where the icache case can actually fail (D34 family). |
| D63 | **Architecture-conformance battery (M27).** Closes D5's "no in-kernel test harness" clause and satisfies `docs/hardware/01` "Porting Rules" 5 (*"Pass kernel architecture tests"*), which previously had no tests to pass. `kernel/arch-conformance` is written against the `tessera-karch` traits only (so it cannot test one port's internals) and run identically by **both** kernel binaries: nine cases — map/translate, W^X refusal, remap rejection, protect, unmap, frame ops, direct map, instruction-cache coherence, context switch — reporting through `kcore::verdict` (D58) with append-only `DemoId` 30–38. Both ports pass 9/9. Its failures feed `DEMOS_FAILED` explicitly, so a port failing the porting-layer contract fails the build (the battery renders its own verdict lines and would otherwise bypass `report`). The per-arch checks that accumulated in the AArch64 boot glue during bring-up were deleted in favour of it. **Scope:** only cases needing the porting layer alone; scheduler-preemption-under-tick and IPC-round-trip cases wait for EL0, when both ports have `kcore`'s object graph standing. | Preemption/IPC conformance cases once EL0 lands on both ports (D61). |
| D64 | **`kcore` has zero x86-isms (M27).** The four leaks the AArch64 port surfaced, all closed: `USER_ADDRESS_MAX` (was the x86-64 48-bit canonical split hard-coded in neutral code) → `AddressSpaceOps` associated const, per-arch (x86-64 2^47, AArch64 `TTBR0` span from `TCR_EL1.T0SZ`); the cycle counter → `CpuOps::counter_serialized`/`counter_hz`, so `event::set_clock` and `trace::set_epoch` no longer receive a value named `read_tsc` (unit unspecified — TSC tracks the core clock, `CNTVCT_EL0` the system-counter rate, so `counter_hz` is `Some` on AArch64 and `None` on x86-64 where the rate isn't architecturally discoverable); `elf::parse` takes a `Machine` parameter (`ElfError::NotX86_64` → `WrongMachine`) so the loader accepts the architecture the *caller* names rather than assuming its own; and `kernel_rsp` → `kernel_stack_top` in the neutral `prepare_resume` signature (`karch-x86_64` keeps the name — there it really is RSP). A grep of `kcore/src` now finds no x86 register, TSC, paging constant, or hard-coded machine type outside explanatory comments. | None — completed cleanup. |
| D65 | **AArch64 B7 (context switch) measured under QEMU (M27).** The one Stage-0 primitive budget the EL1 port can measure honestly — B1–B11 otherwise need syscalls/IPC/the executive, which arrive with EL0. A ping-pong between two kernel contexts brackets exactly two `ContextSwitch::switch` calls per sample, reported in **nanoseconds** (not raw ticks — `CNTVCT_EL0` runs at ~62.5 MHz under QEMU, far coarser than the core clock the TSC tracks, so raw counts would be tiny and incomparable with the x86-64 rig). Same QEMU-certified-regression-only tier as the x86-64 numbers (D34/D56): a tripwire against regression, never an R1 measurement. | Bare-metal R1 measurement of B1–B11 on AArch64 reference hardware (D34/D56). |
| D66 | **ISL union wire codegen (M28).** Closes the union part of D10: `strict`/`flexible` unions now generate a real Rust enum with canonical `WireEncode`/`WireDecode`, proven end to end by `union_variant.isl` + its conformance golden through both build systems.
